# Seed 43 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - not small: 12% smaller, the knee moved again (Iteration 215)

`seed 43 tag2 9h then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --frontier
--cpus 2,8`, commit 9b96930b (the after-seed-42 batch: short branches kept
long, the surrogate screen, fsys 0-4, threads, READ-LINE faster), started
02:41; the evolution 02:44-09:34 (6 h 51 min, generations 0-153), the front
measured again by 09:52, the comparison packed by 11:11. 38,796 designs made,
31,957 measured (31,775 alive), 6,839 screened - not measured.

The comparison's own verdict (five measures): fastest -1.9%, smallest +12.26%,
memory +4 KB, hypervolume +22.4%, best Q +9.3% - not small. The Q-best design
of all runs is seed 43's fc09f6373d: 0.203 at 25,964 bytes, 180 KB, Q 0.210 of
s6's (seed 42's 9a3de58ebe now 0.217 at 26,472). The smallest of all runs is
seed 43's add5ec8937, 16,136 bytes (speed 1.363); the fastest is still seed
42's 28677d13b1 (0.185 at 31,608). All 73 designs on the front of all runs are
in seed 43's database (carried in or its own).

From the run's database (Iteration 215):

- Deaths: 182, every one "reach limit at scale 0 - not run"; none from a short
  branch (converted again since Iteration 199), none on the corpus.
- The screen: 6,839 of the 37,088 children from generation 5 skipped (18.4%);
  805 measured anyway (audited), none of them on the final front.
- Children by the try that made them: mutation 57.3%, crossover 31.4%,
  borrowed 11.3% (the last fifth of generations: crossover 32.0%) at XOVER
  0.25. Iteration 213 asked to verify the explanation (remade repeats): one try
  from the final front against the run's 38,796 designs repeats 24.6% of the
  time mutation-only and 0.5% crossover; the loop remakes a repeat, so the
  children kept should be 30.6% crossover - as found. A generation kept a
  median of 249 new designs of its 256 tries (at least 239).
- Via the attic: 5,797 children; 11 of the 165 on the final front.
- The final front, 165 designs, by the try that made them: mutation 120,
  borrowed 35, crossover 9, carried 1.
- threads: 16 chains (threads=3) on 121 of the 165, the dump's 32 on 44 -
  never 64 or 128; of the 90 without klookup, 83 at 16.
- fsys: 2 on 84, 3 on 44, 4 on 4, 0 on 33. The small third (16,136-18,445
  bytes): 3 on 44, 4 on 4, 2 on 7; the fast third (23,939-34,382): 2 on 49, 0
  on 6. At 9b96930b fsys 4 was fsys 3 with dead bytes (the rebind fault,
  Iteration 211): its 4 designs ran unbuffered.
- 5,667 designs made an hour (4,668 measured); seed 42 3,000.

Checked here with the after-seed-43 batch applied (Iteration 215): every
design of the run's front and of the front of all runs alive with the new code
- 175 of 175 (the corpus, kernel.img byte for byte, far); the 4 fsys 4
designs' images 8-16 bytes smaller - their buffered WRITE bound at last; the
standard suite on 12 of the front (fsys 0, 2, 3 and 4; threads 16 and 32): 0
differ from s6 (s6 2,119 tests, 0 failed, 14 of 14 files whole).

---

# Evolved VM designs

31957 designs evaluated, 31775 alive, 6839 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): fc09f6373d, speed 0.189, binary + image 25,964, memory 180 KB - Q 0.196 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
